\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1})\geq\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2})+\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1}),\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2}\rangle+\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(S73)

and as in
Nesterov, 2003
, the convexity and
\beta
-RSS condition jointly imply that

\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2})-\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1})\geq\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1}),\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1}\rangle+\frac{1}{2\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(S74)

Combining these two inequalities, we have that

\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2}),\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2}\rangle\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\beta}\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2})\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(S75)

which is also known as the restricted correlated gradient condition in
Han et al., 2022
. Additonally, by definition, we immediately have
\alpha\leq\beta
.

Step 2. (Upper bound of
E^{(s+1)}-E^{(s)}
)

By definition,

\begin{split}E^{(s+1)}&=\sum_{i=1}^{6}\left\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s+1)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s+1)}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)}-[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{R}_{1}^{(s+1)\top},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}^{(s+1)\top}]\!]\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{6}\left\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s+1)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\left\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)}-[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{R}_{1}^{(s)\top},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}^{(s)\top}]\!]\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(S76)

Note that
\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s+1)}=\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\eta\nabla_{\mathbf{U}_{i}}\mathcal{L}^{(s)}-\eta a\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}(\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}})
, for
i=1,\dots,2d
. Then,

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s+1)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\eta^{2}\underbrace{\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})_{(i)}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{(s)}+a\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}(\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}}_{T_{1i}}\\
&-2\eta\underbrace{\left\langle\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})_{(i)}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{(s)}\right\rangle}_{T_{2i}}-2\eta a\underbrace{\left\langle\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)},\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}(\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}})\right\rangle}_{T_{3i}}.\end{split}

(S77)

For
T_{1i}
,
i=1,\dots,6
,

\begin{split}T_{1i}&\leq 2\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})_{(i)}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+2a^{2}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}(\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\leq 4\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})_{(i)}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+4\|[\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})]_{(i)}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+2a^{2}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\leq 4\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)}_{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\otimes_{j=1,n\neq i}^{2d}\mathbf{U}_{j}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\left(\xi^{2}+\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\\
&+2a^{2}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\leq 5\bar{\sigma}^{2}b^{-2}\left(\xi^{2}+\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)+3a^{2}b^{2}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}:=Q_{i,2}.\end{split}

(S78)

For
T_{2i}
and
T_{3i}
,
i=1,\dots,6
,

\begin{split}T_{2i}&=\left\langle\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{(s)\top}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\mathbf{V}_{i}^{(s)\top},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})_{(i)}\right\rangle\\
&=\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)};\mathbf{U}_{1}^{(s)},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\dots,\mathbf{U}_{2d}^{(s)},]\!],\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})\right\rangle=\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})\right\rangle\end{split}

(S79)

and

\begin{split}&T_{2i}+a\cdot T_{3i}=\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)},a\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}(\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}})\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})\right\rangle+a\left\langle\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)},\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\right\rangle\\
\geq&\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})\right\rangle+\frac{a}{4}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{a}{4}E^{(s)}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}:=Q_{i,1}.\end{split}

(S80)

Therefore, we have

\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s+1)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-2\eta Q_{i,1}+\eta^{2}Q_{i,2}.

(S81)

Similarly, we can show that

\left\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s+1)}-[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{R}_{1}^{(s)\top},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{2d}^{(s)\top}]\!]\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\left\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)}-[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{R}_{1}^{(s)\top},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{2d}^{(s)\top}]\!]\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-2\eta Q_{S,1}+\eta^{2}Q_{S,2}

(S82)

where

Q_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}},1}:=\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})\right\rangle
,

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}^{(s)}=[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{U}_{1}^{(s)}\mathbf{R}_{1}^{(s)\top},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}^{(s)}\mathbf{R}_{6}^{(s)\top}]\!]
, and

Q_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}},2}:=4b^{12}(\xi^{2}+\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2})
.

Therefore, we have

E^{(s+1)}\leq E^{(s)}-2\eta\left(Q_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}},1}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}Q_{i,1}\right)+\eta^{2}\left(Q_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}},2}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}Q_{i,2}\right).

(S83)

Step 3. Lower bound of
Q_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}},1}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}Q_{i,1}

By definition, we have

\begin{split}Q_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}},1}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}Q_{i,1}=&\left\langle 7\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}^{(s)}-\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})\right\rangle\\
&+a\sum_{i=1}^{6}\left(\frac{1}{4}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}E^{(s)}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\\
=&\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})\right\rangle\\
&+a\sum_{i=1}^{6}\left(\frac{1}{4}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}E^{(s)}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right),\end{split}

(S84)

where
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}^{(s)}-\sum_{i=1}^{6}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})
. By Lemma
S8
,

\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}\leq B_{2}B_{3}^{3}+6B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}^{5/2}+15B_{1}^{2}B_{2}B_{3}^{2}+20B_{1}^{3}B_{2}B_{3}^{3/2}+15B_{1}^{4}B_{2}B_{3}+6B_{1}^{5}B_{3}

(S85)

where

\begin{split}B_{1}&:=\max_{1\leq i\leq 6}\{\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\textup{op}},\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\|_{\textup{op}}\},~B_{2}:=\max_{1\leq i\leq 6}\{\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)}_{(i)}\|_{\textup{op}},\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}^{*}\|_{\textup{op}}\},\\
B_{3}&:=\max_{1\leq i\leq 6}\{\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2},\|\mathbf{H}^{(s)}_{i}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\}.\end{split}

(S86)

Since
B_{1}\leq 1.01
,
B_{2}\leq 1.01
and
B_{3}\leq E^{(s)}\leq c_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\rho^{-2}
, we have
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\alpha\beta^{-1}E^{(s)}/4
.

By (
S75
), the first term on the right hand side of (
S84
) can be further bounded as

\begin{split}&\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})\right\rangle=\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\rangle\\
&+\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\right\rangle+\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\right\rangle\\
&\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(i)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\beta}\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(i)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\\
&-\left|\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\right\rangle\right|.\end{split}

(S87)

In addition, we have that for any
c_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(i)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\beta\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{\alpha E^{(s)}}{4}\end{split}

(S88)

and

\begin{split}&\left|\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\right\rangle\right|\\
=&\left|\left\langle 7\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}^{(s)}-\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\right\rangle\right|\\
\leq&\left|\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\right\rangle\right|+\sum_{i=1}^{6}\left|\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\right\rangle\right|\\
\leq&\xi\cdot\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)}-[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{R}_{1}^{(s)\top},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}^{(s)\top}]\!]\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\mathbf{U}_{1}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}\cdots\|\mathbf{U}_{6}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}\\
+&\xi\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)}_{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\otimes_{j\neq i}\mathbf{U}_{j}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}\\
\leq&\xi\sqrt{E^{(s)}}\left(1.01^{6}\times 7\right)\leq 14c_{1}E^{(s)}+\frac{1}{4c_{1}}\xi^{2},\end{split}

(S89)

for any
c_{1}>0
. Thus, we have

\begin{split}&\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}^{(s)},\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})\right\rangle\\
\geq&\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(i)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(i)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-(\alpha/4+14c_{1})E^{(s)}-\frac{1}{4c_{1}}\xi^{2}.\end{split}

(S90)

Now, applying Lemma
S9
, we have

E^{(s)}\leq b^{-12}(64+24\underline{\sigma}^{-2}C_{1})\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+2C_{1}b^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(S91)

Combining these inequalities, by setting
c_{1}=\alpha b^{12}/112
, we have

\begin{split}&Q_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}},1}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}Q_{i,1}\\
\geq&\left(\frac{\alpha b^{12}}{4}-14c_{1}\right)E^{(s)}+\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4c_{1}}\xi^{2}\\
&+\frac{a}{8}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\frac{\alpha b^{12}}{8}E^{(s)}+\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-28\alpha^{-1}b^{12}\xi^{2}+\frac{a}{8}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(S92)

Step 4. Convergence analysis of
E^{(s)}
and verification of conditions

In the following, we combine all the results in previous steps to establish the error bound for
E^{(s)}
and
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}
. Plugging in
b=\overline{\sigma}^{1/7}
,
a=C\alpha\rho^{-2}\overline{\sigma}
to
Q_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}},2}
and
Q_{i,2}
, we have

\begin{split}&Q_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}},2}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}Q_{i,2}\\
\leq&5\overline{\sigma}^{12/7}[\xi^{2}+\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}]+\frac{C\alpha^{2}\overline{\sigma}^{16/7}}{\rho^{4}}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(S93)

Combining the results above, as
\eta=\eta_{0}\beta^{-1}\rho^{-2}\overline{\sigma}^{-12/7}
for any
\eta_{0}>1/25088
, we have

\begin{split}E^{(s+1)}\leq&\left(1-\frac{\alpha b^{-12}\eta}{8}\right)E^{(s)}\\
+&\left(184\overline{\sigma}^{12/7}-\frac{\eta}{2\beta}\right)\|\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)})-\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\left(\frac{\eta}{2c_{1}}+C\overline{\sigma}_{1}^{12/7}\eta^{2}\right)\xi^{2}\\
+&\left(\frac{C\eta^{2}\alpha^{2}\overline{\sigma}}{\rho^{4}}-\frac{C\eta\alpha\overline{\sigma}^{6/7}}{\rho^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\rho^{-2})E^{(s)}+\rho^{2}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}\end{split}

(S94)

By induction, we have that for any
i=1,2,\dots
,

E^{(s)}\leq(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\rho^{-2})^{s}E^{(0)}+C\rho^{2}\alpha^{-2}\overline{\sigma}^{12/7}\xi^{2}.

(S95)

For the error bound of
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}\|_{\text{F}}
, by Lemma
S9
,

\begin{split}&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(s)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq C\overline{\sigma}^{12/7}E^{(i)}\\
\leq&C\overline{\sigma}^{12/7}(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\rho^{-2})^{i}E^{(0)}+C\rho^{2}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}\\
\leq&C\rho^{2}(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\rho^{-2})^{i}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+C\rho^{2}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}.\end{split}

(S96)

Finally, we show that conditions
E^{(s)}\leq
hold. Since
\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(0)\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(0)}=b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}
for
i=1,\dots,2d
, by Lemma
S9
and initialization bound
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\leq C\underline{\sigma}\alpha^{1/2}\beta^{-1/2}
, we have

E^{(0)}\leq(C\overline{\sigma}^{12/7}+C\underline{\sigma}^{-2})\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq C\overline{\sigma}^{-5/7}\rho^{2}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq C\alpha\beta^{-1}\rho^{-2}.

(S97)

Based on the recursive relationship in (
S96
), by induction it is easy to check that
E^{(s)}\leq C\overline{\sigma}^{2/7}\alpha\beta^{-1}\rho^{-2}
for all
s\geq 1
. In other words, as
\alpha\beta^{-1}\leq 1
and
\rho^{2}\geq 1
, we have
E^{(s)}\leq Cb^{2}
, which further implies that

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}&\leq\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}+\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}\\
&\leq b+\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{(s)}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{i}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}\leq(1+c_{b})b\end{split}

(S98)

and for
i=1,\dots,2d
,

\begin{split}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)}_{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}&\leq\|\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(s)\top}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}_{(i)}\otimes_{j\neq i}\mathbf{O}_{j}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)}_{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(s)\top}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}_{(i)}\otimes_{j\neq i}\mathbf{O}_{j}^{(s)}\|_{\text{op}}\\
&\leq\overline{\sigma}b^{-6}+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(s)}_{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(s)\top}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}_{(i)}\otimes_{j\neq i}\mathbf{O}_{j}^{(s)}\|_{\text{F}}\leq(1+c_{b})b.\end{split}

(S99)

∎

Next, we present some auxiliary lemmas for the proof of local convergence.

Lemma S8
.

Consider the case of
d=3
. Suppose that
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}=[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{U}_{1}^{*},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}^{*}]\!]
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}=[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{U}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}]\!]
. For
\mathbf{R}_{i}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{i}}
and
i=1,\dots,6
, let

\begin{split}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}=&[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{U}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{i-1},\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i},\mathbf{U}_{i+1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}]\!],~~\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}=[\![([\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}]\!]);\mathbf{U}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}]\!]\\
\mathbf{H}_{i}=&\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}-\mathbf{U}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{\top},~~\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}-[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}]\!],~~\text{and}~~\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{6}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}).\end{split}

(S100)

Then, we have that

\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}\|_{\textup{F}}\leq B_{2}B_{3}^{3}+6B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}^{5/2}+15B_{1}^{2}B_{2}B_{3}^{2}+20B_{1}^{3}B_{2}B_{3}^{3/2}+15B_{1}^{4}B_{2}B_{3}+6B_{1}^{5}B_{3}.

(S101)

where

\begin{split}B_{1}&:=\max_{1\leq i\leq 6}\{\|\mathbf{U}_{i}\|_{\textup{op}},\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\|_{\textup{op}}\},~B_{2}:=\max_{1\leq i\leq 6}\{\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}\|_{\textup{op}},\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}^{*}\|_{\textup{op}}\},\\
B_{3}&:=\max_{1\leq i\leq 6}\{\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2},\|\mathbf{H}_{i}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\}.\end{split}

(S102)

Proof.

Since
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}=[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$};\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}]\!]+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}
, we have

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}=[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{U}_{1}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{6}]\!]+[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}};\mathbf{U}_{1}^{*},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}^{*}]\!].

(S103)

For the first term on the right hand of the above equation, we have

\begin{split}&[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{U}_{1}+\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}+\mathbf{H}_{6}\mathbf{R}_{6}]\!]\\
=&\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}\mathbf{U}_{j}\times_{i}\mathbf{H}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{1}\\
=&\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}\mathbf{U}_{j}\times_{i}(\mathbf{U}^{*}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i})+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{1}\\
=&\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}-5\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{1}\end{split}

(S104)

where

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{1}=\sum_{i\neq j}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{k=1,k\neq i,k\neq j}^{6}\mathbf{U}_{k}\times_{i}\mathbf{H}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}\times_{j}\mathbf{H}_{j}\mathbf{R}_{j}+\cdots+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{i=1}^{6}\mathbf{H}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}.

(S105)

For the second term, we have

[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}};\mathbf{U}_{1}^{*},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}^{*}]\!]=(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{i=1}^{6}\mathbf{R}_{i})\times_{i=1}^{6}(\mathbf{H}_{i}+\mathbf{U}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{\top})=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{2}

(S106)

where

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{2}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\times_{i=1}^{6}\mathbf{H}_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\times_{j\neq i}\mathbf{H}_{j}\times_{i}\mathbf{U}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{\top}+\cdots+\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\times_{j\neq i}\mathbf{U}_{j}\mathbf{R}_{j}^{\top}\times_{i}\mathbf{H}_{i}.

(S107)

Then, it follows that

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{i}-6\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}+(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{1}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{2})

(S108)

and

\begin{split}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}&=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{1}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{2}\\
&=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}\times_{i=1}^{6}\mathbf{H}_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}\times_{j\neq i}\mathbf{H}_{j}\times_{i}\mathbf{U}_{i}+\cdots+\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\times_{j\neq i}\mathbf{U}_{j}\mathbf{R}_{j}^{\top}\times_{i}\mathbf{H}_{i}.\end{split}

(S109)

Hence, we have

\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}\|_{\text{F}}\leq B_{2}B_{3}^{3}+6B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}^{5/2}+15B_{1}^{2}B_{2}B_{3}^{2}+20B_{1}^{3}B_{2}B_{3}^{3/2}+15B_{1}^{4}B_{2}B_{3}+6B_{1}^{5}B_{3}.

(S110)

∎

Lemma S9
.

Consider the case of
d=3
. Suppose
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}=[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{U}_{1}^{*},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}^{*}]\!]
,
\mathbf{U}^{*\top}_{i}\mathbf{U}_{i}=\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}
,
i=1,2,3
,
\bar{\sigma}=\max_{1\leq i\leq 6}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}_{(i)}\|_{\textup{op}}
, and
\underline{\sigma}=\min_{1\leq i\leq 6}\sigma_{r_{i}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}_{(i)})
. Let
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}=[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{U}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}]\!]
be another Tucker low-rank tensor with
\|\mathbf{U}_{i}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq(1+c_{0})
, and
\max_{1\leq i\leq 6}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq(1+c_{0})\bar{\sigma}
for some constant
c_{0}>0
. Define

E:=\min_{\mathbf{R}_{i}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{i}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}]\!]\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\right).

(S111)

Then, we have

\begin{split}&E\leq(64+24\underline{\sigma}^{-2}C_{1})b^{-12}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+2C_{1}b^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\min_{\mathbf{R}_{i}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{i}}}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}.\\
&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq 7b^{12}(1+C_{2}^{2}\overline{\sigma}^{2}b^{-14})E.\end{split}

(S112)

where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are some universal constants.

Proof.

First, note that

\|[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}]\!]-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\|_{\text{F}}=b^{-6}\|[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{U}_{1}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{6}]\!]-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}.

(S113)

Then, by the inequality of means, we can

\begin{split}&\|[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{U}_{1}+\mathbf{U}_{1}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{1}-\mathbf{U}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{6}+\mathbf{U}_{6}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{6}-\mathbf{U}_{6}]\!]-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\left\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})+\sum_{i=1}^{6}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{i}(\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i})\times_{j\neq i}\mathbf{U}_{j}+\cdots+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{i=1}^{6}(\mathbf{U}^{*}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i})\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&64b^{-12}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+64b^{-12}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{i}(\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i})\times_{j\neq i}\mathbf{U}_{j}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\cdots+64b^{-12}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{i=1}^{6}(\mathbf{U}^{*}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&64b^{-12}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+64b^{-12}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\otimes_{j\neq i}\mathbf{U}_{j}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+64b^{-12}\sum_{i\neq j}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\otimes_{k\neq i,k\neq j}\mathbf{U}_{k}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{U}_{j}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{j}-\mathbf{U}_{j}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+\cdots\\
&+(64/6)b^{-12}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\otimes_{j\neq i}(\mathbf{U}_{j}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{j}-\mathbf{U}_{j})\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&64b^{-12}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+b^{-12}\Bigg[64\bar{\sigma}^{2}(1+c_{0})^{12}+160\bar{\sigma}^{2}(1+c_{0})^{10}(2+c_{0})^{2}+\frac{640}{3}\bar{\sigma}^{2}(1+c_{0})^{8}(2+c_{0})^{4}+160\bar{\sigma}^{2}(1+c_{0})^{6}\\
&(2+c_{0})^{6}+64\bar{\sigma}^{2}(1+c_{0})^{4}(2+c_{0})^{8}+\frac{32}{3}\bar{\sigma}^{2}(1+c_{0})^{2}(2+c_{0})^{10}\Bigg]\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(S114)

Since the above inequality holds for any orthogonal matrices
\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}
, it follows that

E\leq 64b^{-12}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\overline{\sigma}^{2}b^{-14}C_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\min_{\mathbf{R}_{i}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{i}}}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(S115)

By Lemma E.2 in
Han et al., 2022
,

\min_{\mathbf{R}_{i}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{i}}}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq 2\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+4\underline{\sigma}^{-2}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(S116)

which implies that

E\leq(64+24\underline{\sigma}^{-2}C_{1})\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+2C_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\min_{\mathbf{R}_{i}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{i}}}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(S117)

For the second inequality, denote the optimal rotation matrices by

(\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6})=\argmin_{\mathbf{R}_{i}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{i}}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{U}_{i}-\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\mathbf{R}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*};\mathbf{R}_{1}^{\top},\cdots,\mathbf{R}_{6}^{\top}]\!]\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right\}.

(S118)

Let
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}-[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}]\!]
and
\mathbf{H}_{i}=\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}-\mathbf{U}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{\top}
. Then, we have

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}=(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}+[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{R}_{6}]\!])\times_{i=1}^{6}(\mathbf{H}_{i}+\mathbf{U}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}^{\top})

(S119)

and it follows that

\begin{split}&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\times_{i=1}^{6}\mathbf{U}_{i}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{j\neq i}\mathbf{U}_{j}\times_{i}\mathbf{H}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}+\cdots+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{i=1}^{6}\mathbf{H}_{i}\mathbf{R}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&b^{6}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\|_{\text{F}}+\Bigg[\bar{\sigma}(1+c_{0})^{6}+\frac{5}{2}\bar{\sigma}(1+c_{0})^{5}(2+c_{0})+\frac{10}{3}\bar{\sigma}(1+c_{0})^{4}(2+c_{0})^{2}\\
&+\frac{5}{2}\bar{\sigma}(1+c_{0})^{3}(2+c_{0})^{3}+\bar{\sigma}(1+c_{0})^{2}(2+c_{0})^{4}+\frac{1}{6}\bar{\sigma}(1+c_{0})(2+c_{0})^{5}\Bigg]\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{H}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}\\
=&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\|_{\text{F}}+C_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{H}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}.\end{split}

(S120)

Thus, we have

\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq 7b^{12}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+7C_{2}^{2}b^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{6}\|\mathbf{H}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(S121)

∎

S2.2
Statistical Convergence Analysis

In this appendix, we present the stochastic properties of the time series data. The main technique is the martingale-based concentration inequalities introduced in Appendix
S1
.

Proof of Theorem
6
.

The proof of Theorem
6
follows the deterministic computational convergence analysis in Theorem
5
. It suffices to show that the RSC, RSS and deviation bound conditions hold with high probability.

By Lemmas
S10
and
S11
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]-C\exp(-C\max_{1\leq i\leq d}p_{i})
, the empirical loss function
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
satisfies the RSC-
\alpha_{\text{RSC}}
and RSS-
\beta_{\text{RSS}}
conditions, and

\xi(r_{1},\dots,r_{2d})\lesssim\kappa^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{2d}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{2d}p_{i}r_{i}}{T}}.

(S122)

By Theorem
5
, we have that, for all
i=1,2,\dots
,

\begin{split}&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(i)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\lesssim&~\rho^{2}(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha_{\text{RSC}}\beta_{\text{RSS}}^{-1}\rho^{-2})^{i}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\rho^{2}\alpha_{\text{RSC}}^{-2}\xi(r_{1},\dots,r_{2d}).\end{split}

(S123)

Hence, when

I\gtrsim\frac{\log(\alpha_{\text{RSC}}\beta^{-1}_{\text{RSS}}\xi^{2}(r_{1},\dots,r_{2d}))-\log(\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2})}{\log(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha_{\text{RSC}}\beta^{-1}_{\text{RSS}}\rho^{-2})},

(S124)

the optimization error is absorbed by the statistical error, so

\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(I)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\lesssim\rho^{2}\alpha_{\text{RSC}}^{-2}\kappa^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{2d}p_{i}r_{i}+\prod_{i=1}^{2d}r_{i}}{T}}.

(S125)

∎

In the following, we prove the restricted strong convexity (RSC) and restricted strong smoothness (RSS) conditions. For the least squares loss function
\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})=(2T)^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t}-\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\rangle\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}
, it is easy to check that for any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}\times p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
,

\begin{split}&\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1})-\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2})-\langle\nabla\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2},\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2})\rangle\\
=&\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\rangle\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{1}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{2})_{[S_{2}]}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(S126)

Lemma S10
.

Assume the conditions in Theorem
6
hold and
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\kappa^{2},\kappa^{4})\max_{1\leq i\leq d}p_{i}
. For any tensor
\mathbf{\Delta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}\times p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
of Tucker ranks
(2r_{1},2r_{2},\dots,2r_{2d})
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\kappa^{-2},\kappa^{-4})T]
,

\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\langle\mathbf{\Delta},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\rangle\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq\beta_{\textup{RSS}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2},

(S127)

where
\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}=\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}})/(2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A}))
and
\beta_{\textup{RSS}}=(3\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}}))/(2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A}))
.

Proof.

Denote
\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2d};r_{1},\dots,r_{2d})=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{2d}}:\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\|_{\text{F}}=1,\text{rank}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}_{(i)})=r_{i},~\text{for}~i=1,\dots,2d\}
as the set of low-rank tensors of given dimensions and Tucker ranks.
It suffices to prove the result for
\mathbf{\Delta}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2d};2r_{1},\dots,2r_{2d})
.

For any
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times p}
, denote
R_{T}(\mathbf{M})=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\mathbf{M}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
. Note that
R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})\geq\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})-\sup_{\mathbf{\Delta}}|R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})|
. Similarly to the proof of Lemma
S4
, we have that
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})\geq T\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}})\mu_{\max}^{-1}(\mathcal{A})
.

For any
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
such that
\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\text{F}}=1
and any
t>0
, similarly to Lemma
S4
, by the VMA(
\infty
) representation of VAR(1) model, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}[|R_{T}(\mathbf{M})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{M})|\geq t]\\
\leq&2\exp\left(-\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{\kappa^{4}T\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}})\mu_{\min}^{-2}(\mathcal{A})},\frac{t}{\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}})\mu_{\min}^{-2}(\mathcal{A})}\right)\right).\end{split}

(S128)

Considering an
\epsilon
-covering net
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
for
\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2d};2r_{1},\dots,2r_{2d})
; in other words, for any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2d};r_{1},\dots,r_{2d})
. there exists a
\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}
such that
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}-\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\epsilon
.
By Lemma
S12
, we have that
|\overline{\mathcal{T}}|\leq((6d+3)/\epsilon)^{\prod_{i=1}^{2d}2r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{2d}2p_{i}r_{i}}
. Then, for some small
\epsilon
, the deviation bound between
R_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}_{[S_{2}]})
and its expection can be bounded uniformly over
\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2d};2r_{1},\dots,2r_{2d})
by

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\scalebox{0.75}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2d};r_{1},\dots,r_{2d})}|R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})|\geq t\right]\\
\leq&2\exp\left[C\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2d}2r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{2d}2p_{i}r_{i}\right)-\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{\kappa^{4}T\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}})\mu_{\min}^{-2}(\mathcal{A})},\frac{t}{\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}})\mu_{\min}^{-2}(\mathcal{A})}\right)\right].\end{split}

(S129)

Letting
t=T\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{e}})\mu_{\max}^{-1}(\mathcal{A})/2
, for
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\kappa^{-4},\kappa^{-2})(\prod_{i=1}^{2d}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{2d}p_{i}r_{i})
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{\Delta}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{2d};r_{1},\dots,r_{2d})}|R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})|\geq\frac{T\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})}{2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})}\right]\leq C\exp\left[-c\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2d}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{2d}p_{i}r_{i}\right)\right]

(S130)

and thus

\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\langle\mathbf{\Delta},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\rangle\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})}{2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})}\right]\leq C\exp\left[-c\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2d}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{2d}p_{i}r_{i}\right)\right].

(S131)

Similarly,
R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})\leq\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})+\sup_{\mathbf{\Delta}}|R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})|
and
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{[S_{2}]})\leq T\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})\mu_{\min}^{-1}(\mathcal{A})
. Therefore, the deviation above implies that with high probability,
T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\langle\mathbf{\Delta},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\rangle\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq(3\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}}))/(2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A}))
.
∎

Next, we prove the deviation bound for
\xi(r_{1},\dots,r_{2d})
. For the least squares loss function
\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})=(2T)^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t}-\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\rangle\|_{\text{F}}^{2}
, it is clear that
\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*})=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t}
.

Lemma S11
.

Assume conditions in Theorem
6
hold and
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\kappa^{-4},\kappa^{-2})\max_{1\leq i\leq d}p_{i}
. With probability at least
1-\exp[-C(\prod_{i=1}^{2d}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{2d}p_{i}r_{i})]
,

\begin{split}\xi(r_{1},\dots,r_{2d}):=&\sup_{\|\scalebox{0.75}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}\|_{\textup{F}}=1,\mathbf{U}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{i}=\mathbf{I}_{r_{i}}}\left\langle\nabla\overline{\mathcal{L}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}),[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{U}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{2d}]\!]\right\rangle\\
\lesssim&~\kappa^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{2d}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{2d}p_{i}r_{i}}{T}},\end{split}

(S132)

where
M_{1}=\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})/\mu_{\min}^{1/2}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we consider the case of
d=3
, and the result can be easily extended to the general case of a fixed
d>3
.

Denote
\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{6}}:\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\|_{\text{F}}=1,\text{rank}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}_{(i)})=r_{i},~\text{for}~i=1,\dots,6\}
as the set of low-rank tensors of given dimensions and Tucker ranks. By definition,

\xi(r_{1},\dots,r_{6})=\sup_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\right\rangle.

(S133)

First, we consider an
\epsilon
-net
\overline{\mathcal{T}}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})
for
\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})
. For any tensor
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})
, there exists a tensor
\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})
such that
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}-\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\epsilon
. Obviously,
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}-\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}
is a tensor of Tucker ranks
(2r_{1},\dots,2r_{6})
. Based on the HOSVD of
\mathbf{\Delta}
, we can split
\mathbf{\Delta}
into
64
orthogonal components by splitting each of the factor matrices into two equal-size groups and splitting the core tensor correspondingly. In other words, we can write
\mathbf{\Delta}=\sum_{i=1}^{64}\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}
, where each
\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}
is a tensor of Tucker ranks
(r_{1},\dots,r_{6})
and
\langle\mathbf{\Delta}_{i},\mathbf{\Delta}_{j}\rangle=0
for all
i\neq j
.

By Cauchy’s inequality, as
\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{64}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}
, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{64}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}\leq 8\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}\leq 8\epsilon
. Moreover, since
\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}/\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})
,

\begin{split}\xi(r_{1},\dots,r_{6})&\leq\max_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\right\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{64}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},\frac{\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}}{\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}}\right\rangle\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}\\
&\leq\max_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\right\rangle+8\epsilon\cdot\xi(r_{1},\dots,r_{6}).\end{split}

(S134)

It implies that

\xi(r_{1},\dots,r_{6})\leq(1-8\epsilon)^{-1}\max_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\right\rangle.

(S135)

Next, for any fixed
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{6}}
such that
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\|_{\text{F}}=1
,
\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{e}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}_{[S_{2}]}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\rangle
and we denote
S_{t}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$})=\sum_{s=1}^{t}\langle\mathbf{e}_{s},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}_{[S_{2}]}\mathbf{y}_{s-1}\rangle
and
R_{t}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$})=\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}_{[S_{2}]}\mathbf{y}_{s}\|_{2}^{2}
, for
1\leq t\leq T
.
Similar to Lemma
S5
, by the standard Chernoff bound, for any
z_{1}>0
and
z_{2}>0
,

\mathbb{P}[\{S_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$})\geq z_{1}\}\cap\{R_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$})\leq z_{2}\}]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{z_{1}^{2}}{2\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})z_{2}}\right).

(S136)

Similar to the proof of Lemma
S5
, by Lemma
S6
, we have

\mathbb{P}[R_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$})\geq C\kappa^{2}T\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})\mu_{\min}^{-1}(\mathcal{A})]\leq 2\exp(-CT).

(S137)

Therefore, for any
x>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\right\rangle\geq x\right]\\
\leq&\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\right\rangle\geq(1-8\epsilon)x\right]\\
\leq&|\overline{\mathcal{T}}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})|\cdot\mathbb{P}\left[\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\right\rangle\geq(1-8\epsilon)x\right]\\
\leq&|\overline{\mathcal{T}}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})|\cdot\Big\{\mathbb{P}[\{S_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$})\geq T(1-8\epsilon)x\}\cap\{R_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$})\leq C\kappa^{2}T\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})\mu_{\min}^{-1}(\mathcal{A})\}]\\
+&\mathbb{P}[R_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$})>C\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})\mu_{\min}^{-1}(\mathcal{A})]\Big\}\\
\leq&|\overline{\mathcal{T}}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})|\cdot\left\{\exp\left[-\frac{CTx^{2}}{\kappa^{4}\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})\mu^{-1}_{\min}(\mathcal{A})}\right]+2\exp[-CM_{2}^{-2}\min(\kappa^{-2},\kappa^{-4})T]\right\}.\end{split}

(S138)

By Lemma
S12
,
|\overline{\mathcal{T}}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})|\leq(21/\epsilon)^{\prod_{i=1}^{6}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}p_{i}r_{i}}
. Thus, if we take
\epsilon=0.1
and
x=C\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})\mu_{\min}^{-1}(\mathcal{A})\sqrt{(\prod_{i=1}^{6}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}p_{i}r_{i})/T}
, when
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\kappa^{4},\kappa^{2})\max_{1\leq i\leq 3}p_{i}
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{6};r_{1},\dots,r_{6})}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\circ\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\right\rangle\geq\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{e}})\mu^{-1/2}_{\min}(\mathcal{A})\sqrt{\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{6}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}p_{i}r_{i}}{T}}\right]\\
&\leq\exp\left[-C\left(\prod_{i=1}^{6}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{6}p_{i}r_{i}\right)\right].\end{split}

(S139)

∎

Next, we present the covering number of the set of low-rank tensors.

Lemma S12
.

The
\epsilon
-covering number of the set
\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{d};r_{1},\dots,r_{d}):=\{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}:\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\|_{\text{F}}=1,~\textup{rank}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}_{(i)})\leq r_{i},~i=1,\dots,d\}
is

|\overline{\mathcal{T}}(p_{1},\dots,p_{d};r_{1},\dots,r_{d})|\leq[(3d+3)/\epsilon]^{\prod_{i=1}^{d}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}p_{i}r_{i}}.

(S140)

Proof.

The proof hinges on the covering number for the low-rank matrix developed by
Candes and Plan, 2011
. Recall the HOSVD
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}=[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{U}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{d}]\!]
where
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\|_{\text{F}}=1
and each
\mathbf{U}_{i}
is an orthonormal matrix. We construct an
\epsilon
-net for
\mathcal{T}
by covering the set of
\mathscr{G}
and all
\mathbf{U}_{i}
’s. We take
\overline{G}
to be an
\epsilon/(d+1)
net for
\mathcal{G}
with
\|\overline{G}\|\leq[(3d+3)/\epsilon]^{\prod_{i=1}^{d}r_{i}}
. Next, let
O_{p,r}=\{\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}:\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}=\mathbf{I}_{r}\}
. To cover
O_{p,r}
, it is beneficial to use the
\|\cdot\|_{2,\infty}
norm, defined as

\|\mathbf{M}\|_{2,\infty}=\max_{i}\|\mathbf{M}_{i}\|_{2},

(S141)

where
\mathbf{M}_{i}
denotes the
i
th column of
\mathbf{M}
. Let
Q_{p,r}=\{\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}:\|\mathbf{M}\|_{2,\infty}\leq 1\}
. It is obvious that
O_{p,r}\subset Q_{p,r}
, and thus an
\epsilon/(d+1)
-net
\overline{O}_{p,r}
for
O_{p,r}
obeying
|\overline{O}_{p,r}|\leq[(3d+3)/\epsilon]^{pr}
.

Denote
\overline{T}=\{[\![\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}};\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\dots,\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{d}]\!]:\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}\in\overline{G},\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{i}\in\overline{O}_{p_{i},r_{i}},~i=1,\dots,d\}
and we have
|\overline{T}|\leq|\overline{G}|\times|\overline{O}_{p_{1},r_{1}}|\times\cdots\times|\overline{O}_{p_{d},r_{d}}|=[(3d+3)/\epsilon]^{\prod_{i=1}^{d}r_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}p_{i}r_{i}}
. It suffices to show that for any
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{d};r_{1},\dots,r_{d})
, there exists a
\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\in\overline{T}
such that
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}-\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\epsilon
.

For any fixed
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\in\mathcal{T}(p_{1},\dots,p_{d};r_{1},\dots,r_{d})
, decompose it by HOSVD as
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}=[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$};\mathbf{U}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{U}_{d}]\!]
. Then, there exists
\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}=[\![\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}};\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\dots,\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{d}]\!]
with
\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}\in\overline{G}
,
\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{i}\in\overline{O}_{p_{i},r_{i}}
satisfying that
\|\mathbf{U}_{i}-\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{i}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\epsilon/(d+1)
and
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\epsilon/(d+1)
. This implies that

\begin{split}&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}-\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\|[\![\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}};\mathbf{U}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{d}]\!]\|_{\text{F}}+\|[\![\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}};\mathbf{U}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{d}]\!]\|_{\text{F}}+\cdots+\|[\![\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}};\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{d}-\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{d}]\!]\|_{\text{F}}.\end{split}

(S142)

Since each
\mathbf{U}_{i}
is an orthonormal matrix, the first term is
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}-\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\epsilon/(d+1)
. For the second term, by the all-orthogonal property of
\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}
and orthonormal property of
\mathbf{U}_{2},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{d}
,

\|[\![\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}};\mathbf{U}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{U}_{d}]\!]\|_{\text{F}}=\|\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}\times_{1}(\mathbf{U}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\|_{\text{F}}\leq\|\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}\|_{\text{F}}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}-\overline{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\epsilon/(d+1).

(S143)

Similarly, we can obtain the same upper bound for the other terms, and thus show that
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}-\overline{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\epsilon
.
∎

S2.3
Rank Selection Consistency

This subsection presents the theoretical justification of the proposed ridge-type ratio estimator for rank selection.

Proof of Theorem
7
.

The proof of Theorem
7
consists of two steps. First, the results in Theorem
6
can be readily extended to the rank upper bounds
(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\dots,\bar{r}_{2d})
, as the non-asymptotic analysis can be adapted to any ranks not smaller than the true ranks. Under mild conditions on the signal strengths and the number of iterations, the statistical error bound holds

\|\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\alpha_{\text{RSC}}^{-1}\kappa^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{2d}p_{i}\bar{r}_{i}+\prod_{i=1}^{2d}\bar{r}_{i}}{T}}\lesssim\alpha_{\text{RSC}}^{-1}\kappa^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{p_{\max}\bar{r}_{\max}}{T}}=B.

(S144)

Second, by definition, for any tensor
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{2d}}
,

\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}_{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{p_{j}}\sigma_{j}^{2}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{T}$}_{(i)}),~~1\leq i\leq 2d.

(S145)

That is, the Frobenius norm of the error tensor is equivalent to the
\ell_{2}
norm of the singular values of any matricization. By Mirsky’s singular value inequality,

\sum_{j=1}^{p_{j}}[\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})-\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})]^{2}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{p_{j}}\sigma_{j}^{2}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})=\|\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(S146)

In addition, the
\ell_{\infty}
error bound is smaller than the
\ell_{2}
error bound, and it directly follows the same upper bound

\max_{1\leq j\leq\bar{r}_{i}}|\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})-\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})|\leq\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{r}_{i}}[\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})-\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})]^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\leq\|\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim B.

(S147)

Note that
\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})+s(p_{\max},T)=\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(i)}^{*})+[\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})-\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})]+s(p_{\max},T)
. For
j>r_{i}
, since
\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(i)}^{*})=0
and
\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})-\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(i)}^{*})=o_{p}(s(p_{\max},T))
,
s(p_{\max},T)
is the dominating term in
\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})+s(p_{\max},T)
. For
j\geq r_{i}
, since
\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})-\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(i)}^{*})=o_{p}(s(p_{\max},T))
and
s(p_{\max},T)=o(\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)}))
,
\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})
is the dominating term.

Hence, for
j>r_{i}
, as
T\to\infty
,

\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})+c}{\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})+c}\to\frac{c}{c}=1.

(S148)

For
j<r_{i}
,

\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})+c}{\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})+c}\to\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})}{\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})}.

(S149)

For
j=r_{i}
,

\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})+c}{\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})+c}\to\frac{s(p_{\max},T)}{\sigma_{r_{i}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})}\leq\frac{s(p_{\max},T)}{\underline{\sigma}}=o\left(\min_{1\leq i\leq r_{i}-1}\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})}{\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)})}\right).

(S150)

Combining these two steps, we can conclude the rank selection consistency in this theorem.
∎

S3
ADMM Algorithm for (T)SSN Estimator

This subsection presents the algorithm for the proposed (T)SSN regularized estimator. The algorithm for
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}_{\textup{SN}}
can be developed analogously, while
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}_{\textup{MN}}
can be obtained easily as in
Negahban and Wainwright, 2011
.

The objective function for the estimator
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}_{\textup{SSN}}
in (
25
) can be rewritten as

\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})+\lambda_{\text{SSN}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}\|_{\text{SSN}}=\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})+\lambda_{\text{SSN}}\sum_{k=1}^{2^{d-1}}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{[I_{k}]}\|_{\textup{nuc}},

(S151)

where
\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t}-\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\rangle\|_{\text{F}}^{2}
is the quadratic loss function. In (
S151
), the regularizer
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}\|_{\text{SSN}}
involves
2^{d-1}
nuclear norms
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{[I_{k}]}\|_{\textup{nuc}}
, which are challenging to handle at the same time. A similar difficulty also occurs in low-rank tensor completion, for which
Gandy et al., 2011
applied the alternating direction method of multipliers (ADMM) algorithm
(
Boyd et al., 2011
)
to efficiently separate the different nuclear norms. Borrowing the idea of
Gandy et al., 2011
, we develop an ADMM algorithm for the miminization of (
S151
).

Algorithm 1

ADMM algorithm for (T)SSN estimator

Initialize:
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{C}$}_{k}^{(0)}
,
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k}^{(0)}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}_{\text{MN}}
, for
k=1,\dots,2^{d-1}
, threshold parameter
\gamma

for

j\in\{0,1,\dots,J-1\}

do

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(j+1)}\leftarrow\arg\min\Big\{\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})+\sum_{k=1}^{2^{d-1}}\rho\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k}^{(j)}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{C}$}_{k}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\Big\}

for

k\in\{1,2,\dots,2^{d-1}\}

do

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k}^{(j+1)}\leftarrow\arg\min\Big\{\rho\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(j+1)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{C}$}_{k}^{(j)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\lambda_{\text{SSN}}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k})_{[I_{k}]}\|_{\textup{nuc}}\Big\}

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{C}$}^{(j+1)}_{k}\leftarrow\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{C}$}_{k}^{(j)}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(j+1)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}^{(j+1)}_{k}

end for

end for

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}_{\text{SSN}}\leftarrow\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(J)}

for

i\in\{1,2,\dots,2d\}

do

\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{i}\leftarrow\text{Truncated\_SVD}((\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}_{\text{SSN}})_{(i)},\gamma)

end for

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{G}}$}\leftarrow\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}_{\text{SSN}}\times_{i=1}^{2d}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{i}^{\top}

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}_{\text{TSSN}}\leftarrow\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{G}}$}\times_{i=1}^{2d}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{i}

To separate the
2^{d-1}
nuclear norms in
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}\|_{\text{SSN}}
, for each
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{[I_{k}]}
, we introduce a different dummy variable
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k}
as a surrogate for
\mathscr{A}
, where
k=1,\dots,2^{d-1}
. Then the augmented Lagrangian is

\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{C}$})=\mathcal{L}_{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})+\sum_{k=1}^{2^{d-1}}\Big[\lambda_{\text{SSN}}\|(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k})_{[I_{k}]}\|_{\textup{nuc}}+2\rho\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{C}$}_{k},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k}\rangle+\rho\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\Big],

where
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{C}$}_{k}
are the Lagrangian multipliers, for
k=1,\dots,2^{d-1}
, and
\rho
is the regularization parameter. Then we can iteratively update
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{C}$}_{k}
by the ADMM, as shown in Algorithm
1
.

In Algorithm
1
, the
\mathscr{A}
-update step is an
\ell_{2}
-regularized least squares problem. Similarly to
Gandy et al., 2011
, the
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{W}$}_{k}
-update step can be solved by applying the explicit soft-thresholding operator to the singular values of
(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{C}$}_{k})_{[I_{k}]}
. Both subproblems have close-form solutions. Thus, the miminization of (
S151
) can be solved efficiently.

S4
Interesting Special Cases of the LRTAR Model

We discuss two special cases of the proposed LRTAR model and their connections with the matrix autoregressive model in
Chen et al., 2021
and the tensor factor model in
Chen et al., 2022
.

Example 1
.

For simplicity, we first consider the case with
d=2
, so
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t}\equiv\mathbf{Y}_{t},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t}\equiv\mathbf{E}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}
are matrices. Then the VAR representation in (
14
) becomes

\textup{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{t})=(\mathbf{U}_{4}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{3})\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{[\{3,4\}]}(\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\top}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top})\textup{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{t-1})+\textup{vec}(\mathbf{E}_{t}),

(S152)

and the low-dimensional representation in (
17
) becomes

\mathbf{U}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{t}\mathbf{U}_{4}=\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$},\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\mathbf{U}_{2}\right\rangle+\mathbf{U}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{t}\mathbf{U}_{4},

where
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{4}}
. It is interesting to compare this model with the matrix autoregressive (MAR) model in
Chen et al., 2021
and
Hoff, 2015
, which is defined by

\mathbf{Y}_{t}=\mathbf{B}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{t-1}\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\top}+\mathbf{E}_{t},

(S153)

where
\mathbf{B}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{1}}
and
\mathbf{B}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times p_{2}}
, whose vector form is

\textup{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{t})=(\mathbf{B}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1})\textup{vec}(\mathbf{Y}_{t-1})+\textup{vec}(\mathbf{E}_{t}).

(S154)

It can be easily seen that if
r_{1}=r_{3}=p_{1}
,
r_{2}=r_{4}=p_{2}
,
\mathbf{U}_{3}=\mathbf{I}_{p_{1}}
,
\mathbf{U}_{4}=\mathbf{I}_{p_{2}}
, and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{[\{3,4\}]}=(\mathbf{B}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1})(\mathbf{U}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{1})
, then (
S152
) becomes exactly (
S154
). Thus, the MAR model in (
S153
) can be viewed as a special case of the proposed model without reducing dimensions
p_{i}
’s to
r_{i}
’s and without transforming
\mathbf{Y}_{t}
; see Figure
1
for an illustration.
The above comparison also applies to the general case with
d\geq 3
. The tensor version of the MAR model is considered in
Hoff, 2015
and is defined as

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\times_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{B}_{i}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t},

(S155)

where
\mathbf{B}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{i}\times p_{i}}
for
i=1,\dots,d
. We call (
S155
) the multilinear tensor autoregressive (MTAR) model.
Note that its vector form is

\textup{vec}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t})=(\mathbf{B}_{d}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbf{B}_{1})\textup{vec}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1})+\textup{vec}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t}).

(S156)

Similarly, (
S156
) is a special case of (
14
) with
r_{i}=r_{d+i}=p_{i}
,
\mathbf{U}_{d+i}=\mathbf{I}_{p_{i}}
, for
i=1,\dots,d
, and

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{[S_{2}]}=(\otimes_{i\in S_{1}}\mathbf{B}_{i})(\otimes_{i\in S_{1}}\mathbf{U}_{i})
. Obviously, the number of unknown parameters in the MTAR model,
\sum_{i=1}^{d}p_{i}^{2}
, is much larger than that of the proposed model as shown in (
16
). Also note that
Chen et al., 2021
focuses on the low-dimensional estimation and its asymptotic theory, while
Hoff, 2015
considers a Bayesian estimation method.

Figure 1:
Illustration of the MAR model and the proposed LRTAR model in the case of
d=2
.

Example 2
.

In the special case where
\mathbf{U}_{d+i}=\mathbf{U}_{i}
and
r_{d+i}=r_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,d
, the proposed model may be understood from the perspective of dynamic factor modeling
(
Stock and Watson, 2011
;
Bai and Wang, 2016
)
for tensor-valued time series. Specifically, consider the following model:

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{F}$}_{t}\times_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{U}_{i},\quad\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{F}$}_{t}=\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{F}$}_{t-1}\rangle+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{t},

(S157)

where
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
is the observed tensor-valued time series,
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{F}$}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d}}
represents
\prod_{i=1}^{d}r_{i}
factors, and
\mathbf{U}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{i}\times r_{i}}
are orthonormal matrices for
i=1,\dots,d
. Here
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{F}$}_{t}
follows the tensor autoregression (TAR) with transition tensor
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d}\times r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d}}
and random error
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{t}
.
Note that (
S157
) can be rewritten as

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t}=\left\langle\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{U}_{i}\times_{i=d+1}^{2d}\mathbf{U}_{i},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t-1}\right\rangle+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{t}\times_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{U}_{i}.

Thus, model (
S157
) is a special case of the proposed model with
\mathbf{U}_{d+i}=\mathbf{U}_{i}
and
r_{d+i}=r_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,d
, and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{H}$}_{t}\times_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{U}_{i}
.
Chen et al., 2022
introduces the tensor factor model in the form of
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{F}$}_{t}\times_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{U}_{i}+\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{E}$}_{t}
without an explicit modeling of the latent factors
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{F}$}_{t}
. Hence, model (
S157
) may be regarded as a special tensor factor model with autoregressive dynamic factors, but without any random error in the model equation of
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{Y}$}_{t}
.
